[penguin] output cap clamped hard: max_tokens 256000 -> 118663 (context_window 128000, estimated input 8313)
Now let me understand the setup. The setup.py uses `cythonize` and `numpy.get_include()`. The pyx files use `cimport numpy as n` which is the old numpy C API. Numpy 2.0 changed the C API significantly - functions like `PyArray_DescrFromType`, `PyArray_SimpleNew`, etc. were removed, and macros became functions. Cython needs to be a recent version to support NumPy 2.0 (`cython >= 3.0.11` or so with numpy 2 compat).

Let me check the Cython version installed é the environment. Also check what tests exist Tangent.

Also the `numpy` cimport might use deprecated API. The installed `numpy` headers might already handle the deprecations, but Cython compiled modules using old API attributes will break. Let me check Cython version. With Cython 3.x, `cimport numpy` is fine (numpy Cython API was updated in numpy 2 Cython support).

Actually, for NumPy 2.0, the recommended way is to use `Cython` >= 3.0.8 (I think 3.0.10+, actually 3.0.11) with `numpy` support. Let me check.

Also, the setup.py uses `distutils.extension`, which is removed in Python 3.12+! Python 3.13 has removed distutils entirely. So setup.py needs to use `setuptools` extension instead.

Let me check the environment more carefully first

[tool-c42] read_file ->    349	    def isolate_knot(self):
[tool-c42] read_file ->    350	        '''Return indices of self.points within which the knot (if any)
[tool-c42] read_file ->    351	        appears to lie, according to a simple closure algorithm.
[tool-c42] read_file ->    352	
[tool-c42] read_file ->    353	        This method is experimental and may not provide very good results.
[tool-c42] read_file ->    354	        '''
[tool-c42] read_file ->    355	        if self._cached_isolated is not None:
[tool-c42] read_file ->    356	            return self._cached_isolated
[tool-c42] read_file ->    357	        determinant = self.determinant()
[tool-c42] read_file ->    358	
[tool-c42] read_file ->    359	        from pyknotid.spacecurves import OpenKnot
[tool-c42] read_file ->    360	
[tool-c42] read_file ->    361	        k1 = OpenKnot(self.points, verbose=False)
[tool-c42] read_file ->    362	        start, end = _isolate_open_knot(k1, determinant, 0, len(k1))[1:]
[tool-c42] read_file ->    363	
[tool-c42] read_file ->    364	        if end - start < 0.6 * len(k1):
[tool-c42] read_file ->    365	            self._cached_isolated = (start, end)
[tool-c42] read_file ->    366	            return start, end
[tool-c42] read_file ->    367	
[tool-c42] read_file ->    368	        roll_dist = int(0.25*len(self.points))
[tool-c42] read_file ->    369	        k2 = OpenKnot(n.roll(self.points, roll_dist, axis=0), verbose=False)
[tool-c42] read_file ->    370	        start, end = _isolate_open_knot(k2, determinant, 0, len(k2))[1:]
[tool-c42] read_file ->    371	        print('se', start, end)
[tool-c42] read_file ->    372	        start -= roll_dist
[tool-c42] read_file ->    373	        start %= len(self)
[tool-c42] read_file ->    374	        end -= roll_dist
[tool-c42] read_file ->    375	        end %= len(self)
[tool-c42] read_file ->    376	        print('now', start, end)
[tool-c42] read_file ->    377	        self._cached_isolated = (start, end)
[tool-c42] read_file ->    378	        return start, end
[tool-c42] read_file ->    379	
[tool-c42] read_file ->    380	    def plot_isolated(self, **kwargs):
[tool-c42] read_file ->    381	        '''
[tool-c42] read_file ->    382	        Plots the curve in red, except for the isolated local knot which
[tool-c42] read_file ->    383	        is coloured blue. The local knot is found with self.isolate_knot,
[tool-c42] read_file ->    384	        which may not be reliable or have good resolution.
[tool-c42] read_file ->    385	
[tool-c42] read_file ->    386	        Parameters
[tool-c42] read_file ->    387	        ==========
[tool-c42] read_file ->    388	        **kwargs :
[tool-c42] read_file ->    389	            kwargs are passed directly to :meth:`Knot.plot`.
[tool-c42] read_file ->    390	        '''
[tool-c42] read_file ->    391	        start, end = self.isolate_knot()
[tool-c42] read_file ->    392	        if end < start:
[tool-c42] read_file ->    393	            end, start = start, end
[tool-c42] read_file ->    394	        mus = n.zeros(len(self.points))
[tool-c42] read_file ->    395	        mus[start:end+1] = 0.4
[tool-c42] read_file ->    396	        if end - start > 0.6*len(self) or end == start:
[tool-c42] read_file ->    397	            mus = 0.4 - mus
[tool-c42] read_file ->    398	        self.plot(mus=mus, **kwargs)

[tool-c42] read_file ->    482	    def whitney_index(self):
[tool-c42] read_file ->    483	        '''The degree of the Gauss map mapping a point on the curve to the
[tool-c42] read_file ->    484	        direction of the positive tangent vector at this point.'''
[tool-c42] read_file ->    485	
[tool-c42] read_file ->    486	        points = self.points
[tool-c42] read_file ->    487	        tangents = self.tangents()
[tool-c42] read_file ->    488	
[tool-c42] read_file ->    489	        directions = np.apply_along_axis(
[tool-c42] read_file ->    490	            lambda arr: np.arctan2(arr[1], arr[0]), 1, tangents)
[tool-c42] read_file ->    491	
[tool-c42] read_file ->    492	        directions[directions > np.pi] -= 2*np.pi
[tool-c42] read_file ->    493	        directions[directions < -np.pi] += 2*np.pi
[tool-c42] read_file ->    494	
[tool-c42] read_file ->    495	        jumps = np.roll(directions, -1) - directions
[tool-c42] read_file ->    496	
[tool-c42] read_file ->    497	
[tool-c42] read_file ->    498	        index = np.sum(jumps > np.pi) - np.sum(jumps < -np.pi)
[tool-c42] read_file ->    499	
[tool-c42] read_file ->    500	        return index
[tool-c42] read_file ->    501	
[tool-c42] read_file ->    502	    def arnold_2St_2Jplus(self, **kwargs):
[tool-c42] read_file ->    503	        from pyknotid.invariants import arnold_2St_2Jplus
[tool-c42] read_file ->    504	        gc = self.gauss_code(**kwargs)
[tool-c42] read_file ->    505	        # Do *not* simplify, as this is only a plane curve invariant
[tool-c42] read_file ->    506	        return arnold_2St_2Jplus(gc)
[tool-c42] read_file ->    507	
[tool-c42] read_file ->    508	    def arnold_2St_2Jminus(self, **kwargs):
[tool-c42] read_file ->    509	        from pyknotid.invariants import arnold_2St_2Jminus
[tool-c42] read_file ->    510	        gc = self.gauss_code(**kwargs)
[tool-c42] read_file ->    511	        # Do *not* simplify, as this is only a plane curve invariant
[tool-c42] read_file ->    512	        return arnold_2St_2Jminus(gc)

[tool-18e] read_file ->    121	def do_vectors_intersect(px, py, dpx, dpy,
[tool-18e] read_file ->    122	                                qx, qy, dqx, dqy):
[tool-18e] read_file ->    123	    """Takes four vectors p, dp and q, dq, then tests whether they cross in
[tool-18e] read_file ->    124	    the dp/dq region. Returns this boolean, and the (fractional) point where
[tool-18e] read_file ->    125	    the crossing actually occurs.
[tool-18e] read_file ->    126	    """

[tool-18e] read_file ->    128	        return (0, 0., 0.)
[tool-18e] read_file ->    129	
[tool-18e] read_file ->    130	    t = cross_product(qx - px, qy - py, dqx, dqy) / cross_product(dpx, dpy,
[tool-18e] read_file ->    131	                                                                  dqx, dqy)
[tool-18e] read_file ->    132	    if t < 1.0 and t > 0.0:
[tool-18e] read_file ->    133	        u = cross_product(qx - px, qy - py, dpx, dpy) / cross_product(dpx, dpy,
[tool-18e] read_file ->    134	                                                                      dqx, dqy)

[tool-18e] read_file ->    136	            return (1, t, u)

[tool-2bd] read_file ->      2	SpaceCurve
[tool-2bd] read_file ->      3	----------
[tool-2bd] read_file ->      4	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->      5	The :class:`SpaceCurve` class is the base for all space-curve analysis
[tool-2bd] read_file ->      6	in pyknotid. It provides methods for geometrical manipulation
[tool-2bd] read_file ->      7	(translation, rotation etc.), calculating geometrical characteristics
[tool-2bd] read_file ->      8	such as the writhe, and obtaining topological representations of the
[tool-2bd] read_file ->      9	curve by analysing its crossings in projection.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     10	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     11	pyknotid provides other classes for topological analysis of the curves:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     12	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     13	- :class:`~pyknotid.spacecurves.knot.Knot` for calculating knot invariants of the space-curve.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     14	- :class:`~pyknotid.spacecurves.link.Link` to handle multiple SpaceCurves and calculate linking invariants.

[tool-2bd] read_file ->     45	class SpaceCurve(object):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     46	    '''
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     47	    Class for holding the vertices of a single line, providing helper
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     48	    methods for convenient manipulation and analysis.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     49	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     50	    The methods of this class are largely geometrical (though this
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     51	    includes listing the crossings in projection and extracting a
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     52	    Gauss code etc.). For topological measurements, you should use
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     53	    a :class:`~pyknotid.spacecurves.knot.Knot`.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     54	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     55	    This class deliberately combines methods to do many different kinds
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     56	    of measurements or manipulations. Some of these are externally
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     57	    available through other modules in pyknotid - if so, this is usually
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     58	    indicated in the method docstrings.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     59	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     60	    Parameters
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     61	    ----------
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     62	    points : array-like
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     63	        The 3d points (vertices) of a piecewise
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     64	        linear curve representation
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     65	    verbose : bool
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     66	        Indicates whether the SpaceCurve should print
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     67	        information during processing
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     68	    add_closure : bool
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     69	        If True, adds a final point to the knot near to the start point,
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     70	        so that it will appear visually to close when plotted.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     71	    zero_centroid : bool
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     72	        If True, shifts the coordinates of the points so that their centre
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     73	        of mass is at the origin.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     74	    '''
[tool-2bd] read_file ->     75

[tool-2bd] read_file ->    125	    def roll(self, distance, fix_endpoints=True):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    126	        # if fix_endpoints, adds a point at the end that matches the
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    127	        # point at the start
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    128	        points = self.points
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    129	        if mag(points[-1] - points[0]) < 0.000001 and fix_endpoints:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    130	            points = self.points[:-1]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    131	        points = np.roll(points, distance, axis=0)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    132	        if fix_endpoints:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    133	            points = np.vstack([points, points[:1]])
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    134	        self.points = points

[tool-2bd] read_file ->    145	    def _add_closure(self):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    146	        closing_distance = mag(self.points[-1] - self.points[0])
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    147	        if closing_distance > 0.02:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    148	            self.points = n.vstack((self.points, self.points[:1] -
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    149	                                    0.001*(self.points[0] - self.points[-1])))
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    150	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    151	    def _unwrap_periodicity(self, shape):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    152	        '''
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    153	        Walks along the points of self, assuming that a periodic
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    154	        boundary on a
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    155	        lattice bounded by :param:`shape` has been crossed whenever one
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    156	        point is too far from the previous one. When this occurs,
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    157	        subtracts the lattice vector in this direction.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    158	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    159	        Parameters
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    160	        ----------
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    161	        shape : array-like
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    162	            The x, y, z distances of the periodic boundary.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    163	        '''
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    164	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    165	        dx, dy, dz = shape
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    166	        points = self.points
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    167	        for i in range(1, len(points)):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    168	            prevLine = points[i-1]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    169	            curLine = points[i]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    170	            rest = points[i:]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    171	            change = curLine - prevLine
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    172	            if -1.05*dx < change[0] < -0.95*dx:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    173	                rest[:, 0] += dx
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    174	            if 1.05*dx > change[0] > 0.95*dx:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    175	                rest[:, 0] -= dx
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    176	            if -1.05*dy < change[1] < -0.95*dy:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    177	                rest[:, 1] += dy
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    178	            if 1.05*dy > change[1] > 0.95*dy:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    179	                rest[:, 1] -= dy
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    180	            if -1.05*dz < change[2] < -0.95*dz:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    181	                rest[:, 2] += dz
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    182	            if 1.05*dz > change[2] > 0.95*dz:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    183	                rest[:, 2] -= dz
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    184	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    185	        self.points = points
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    186	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    187	    @classmethod
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    188	    def from_gauss_code(cls, code):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    189	        '''Creates a Knot from the given code, which must be provided as a
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    190	        string and may optionally include crossing orientations (these are
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    191	        actually ignored).'''
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    192	        from pyknotid.representations import Representation
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    193	        if 'a' in code or 'c' in code:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    194	            k = cls(Representation(code).space_curve())
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    195	        else:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    196	            k = cls(Representation.calculating_orientations(code).space_curve())
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    197	        k.rotate((0.05, 0.03, 0.02))
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    198	        return k

[tool-2bd] read_file ->    200	    @classmethod
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    201	    def closing_on_sphere(cls, line, com=(0., 0., 0.)):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    202	        '''Adds new vertices to close the line at its maximum radius,
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    203	        returning a SpaceCurve representing the result.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    204	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    205	        Parameters
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    206	        ----------
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    207	        line : ndarray
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    208	            The points of the line.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    209	        com : iterable
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    210	            Optional additional centre of mass to shift by before closing
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    211	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    212	        '''
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    213	        com = ensure_shape_tuple(com)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    214	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    215	        points = line - n.array(com)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    216	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    217	        start = points[0]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    218	        end = points[-1]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    219	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    220	        start_r = mag(start)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    221	        end_r = mag(end)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    222	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    223	        start_theta = n.arccos(start[2] / start_r)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    224	        end_theta = n.arccos(end[2] / end_r)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    225	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    226	        start_phi = n.arctan2(start[1], start[0])
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    227	        end_phi = n.arctan2(end[1], end[0])
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    228	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    229	        rs = n.linspace(end_r, start_r, 100)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    230	        thetas = n.linspace(end_theta, start_theta, 100)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    231	        phis = n.linspace(end_phi, start_phi, 100)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    232	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    233	        join_points = n.zeros((100, 3))
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    234	        join_points[:, 2] = rs * n.cos(thetas)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    235	        join_points[:, 0] = rs * n.sin(thetas) * n.cos(phis)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    236	        join_points[:, 1] = rs * n.sin(thetas) * n.sin(phis)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    237	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    238	        return cls(n.vstack((points, join_points[1:-1])) + n.array(com))
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    239

[tool-2bd] read_file ->    286	    @classmethod
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    287	    def from_braid_word(cls, word):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    288	        '''
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    289	        Returns a :class:`SpaceCurve` instance formed from the given braid
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    290	        word.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    291	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    292	        The braid word should be of the form 'aAbBcC' (i.e. capitalisation
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    293	        denotes inverse).
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    294	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    295	        Parameters
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    296	        ----------
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    297	        word : str
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    298	            The braid word to interpret.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    299	        '''
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    300	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    301	        num_strands = len(set(word.lower())) + 1
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    302	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    303	        xs = n.arange(num_strands) + 1
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    304	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    305	        y = 0.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    306	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    307	        lines = [[[x, y, 0.]] for x in xs]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    308	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    309	        strands_by_final_index = {strand: lines[strand] for strand in range(num_strands)}
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    310	        strands_by_initial_index = {strand: lines[strand] for strand in range(num_strands)}
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    311	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    312	        for letter in word:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    313	            index = ord(letter.lower()) - ord('a')
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    314	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    315	            sign = '+' if letter.isupper() else '-'
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    316	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    317	            if sign == '+':
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    318	                lines[index].append([index + 1 + 0.5, y + 0.5, 1.])
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    319	                lines[index].append([index + 1 + 1., y + 1., 0.])
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    320	                lines[index + 1].append([index + 1 + 0.5, y + 0.5, -1.])
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    321	                lines[index + 1].append([index + 1, y + 1., 0.])
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    322	            else:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    323	                lines[index + 1].append([index + 1 + 0.5, y + 0.5, 1.])
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    324	                lines[index + 1].append([index + 1, y + 1., 0.])
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    325	                lines[index].append([index + 1 + 0.5, y + 0.5, -1.])
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    326	                lines[index].append([index + 1 + 1., y + 1., 0.])
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    327	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    328	            lines[index], lines[index + 1] = lines[index + 1], lines[index]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    329	            strands_by_final_index[index], strands_by_final_index[index + 1] = strands_by_final_index[index + 1], strands_by_final_index[index]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    330	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    331	            y += 1.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    332	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    333	            for i, line in enumerate(lines):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    334	                if i not in (index, index + 1):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    335	                    lines[i].append([i + 1., y, 0.])
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    336	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    337	        for i, line in enumerate(lines):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    338	            line.append([0., y + i + 1, 0.])
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    339	            line.append([-1*(i + 1), y, 0.])
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    340	            line.append([-1*(i + 1), 0, 0.])
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    341	            line.append([0., -i - 1., 0.])
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    342	            line.append([i + 1., -0.5, 0.])
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    343	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    344	        # indices_by_strand = {strand: index for (strand, index) in strands_by_final_index.items()}
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    345	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    346	        line = []
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    347	        index = 0
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    348	        for i in range(num_strands):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    349	            cur_strand = strands_by_initial_index[index]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    350	            line.extend(cur_strand)

[tool-2bd] read_file ->    408	    def cuaps(self, include_closure=True):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    409	        '''Returns a list of the 'cuaps', where the curve is parallel to the
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    410	        positive x-axis. See D Bar-Natan and R van der Veen, "A
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    411	        polynomial time knot polynomial", 2017.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    412	        '''
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    413	        points = self.points
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    414	        if mag(points[-1] - points[0]) < 0.000001:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    415	            points = points[:-1]  # Avoids a badly defined tangent
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    416	        tangents = np.roll(points, -1, axis=0) - points
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    417	        angles = np.arctan2(tangents[:, 1], tangents[:, 0])
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    418	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    419	        print('angles', angles)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    420	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    421	        cuaps = []
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    422	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    423	        for i in range(len(points) - 2):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    424	            angle = angles[i]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    425	            next_angle = angles[i + 1]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    426	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    427	            if (0 < angle < np.pi / 2.) and (-np.pi / 2. < next_angle < 0):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    428	                cuaps.append([i + 1, -1])
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    429	            elif (-np.pi / 2. < angle < 0) and (0 < next_angle < np.pi / 2.):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    430	                cuaps.append([i + 1, 1])
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    431	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    432	        if include_closure:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    433	            angle = angles[-2]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    434	            next_angle = angles[-1]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    435	            if (0 < angle < np.pi / 2.) and (-np.pi / 2. < next_angle < 0):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    436	                cuaps.append([len(angles) - 1, -1])
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    437	            elif (-np.pi / 2. < angle < 0) and (0 < next_angle < np.pi / 2.):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    438	                cuaps.append([len(angles) - 1, 1])
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    439	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    440	            angle = angles[-1]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    441	            next_angle = angles[0]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    442	            if (0 < angle < np.pi / 2.) and (-np.pi / 2. < next_angle < 0):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    443	                cuaps.append([0, -1])
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    444	            elif (-np.pi / 2. < angle < 0) and (0 < next_angle < np.pi / 2.):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    445	                cuaps.append([0, 1])
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    446	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    447	        cuaps = np.array(cuaps)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    448	        # cuaps[:, -1] *= -1
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    449	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    450	        return cuaps
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    451	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    452	    def raw_crossings(self, mode='use_max_jump', include_closure=True,
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    453	                      recalculate=False, try_cython=True):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    454	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    455	        '''Returns the crossings in the diagram of the projection of the
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    456	        space curve into its z=0 plane.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    457	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    458	        The crossings will be calculated the first time this function
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    459	        is called, then cached until an operation that would change
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    460	        the list (e.g. rotation, or changing ``self.points``).
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    461	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    462	        Multiple modes are available (see parameters) - you should be
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    463	        aware of this because different modes may be vastly slower or
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    464	        faster depending on the type of line.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    465	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    466	        Parameters
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    467	        ----------
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    468	        mode : str, optional
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    469	            One of ``'count_every_jump'`` or ``'use_max_jump'`` or
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    470	            ``'naive'``. In the first case,
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    471	            walking along the line uses information about the length of
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    472	            every step. In the second, it guesses that all steps have the
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    473	            same length as the maximum step length. In the last, no
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    474	            optimisation is made and every crossing is checked.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    475	            The optimal choice depends on the data but is usually
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    476	            ``'use_max_jump'``, which is the default.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    477	        include_closure : bool, optional
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    478	            Whether to include crossings with the
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    479	            line joining the start and end points. Defaults to True.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    480	        recalculate : bool, optional
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    481	            Whether to force a recalculation of the crossing positions.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    482	            Defaults to False.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    483	        try_cython : bool, optional
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    484	            Whether to force the use of the python (as opposed to cython)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    485	            implementation of find_crossings. This will make no difference
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    486	            if the cython could not be loaded, in which case python is already
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    487	            used automatically. Defaults to True.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    488	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    489	        Returns
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    490	        -------
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    491	        array-like
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    492	            The raw array of floats representing crossings, of the
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    493	            form [[line_index, other_index, +-1, +-1], ...], where the
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    494	            line_index and other_index are in arclength parameterised
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    495	            by integers for each vertex and linearly interpolated,
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    496	            and the +-1 represent over/under and clockwise/anticlockwise
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    497	            respectively.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    498	        '''
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    499	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    500	        if not recalculate and self._crossings is not None:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    501	            return self._crossings
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    502	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    503	        if try_cython:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    504	            helpers_module = chelpers
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    505	        else:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    506	            helpers_module = helpers
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    507	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    508	        self._vprint('Finding crossings')
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    509	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    510	        points = self.points
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    511	        segment_lengths = n.roll(points[:, :2], -1, axis=0) - points[:, :2]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    512	        segment_lengths = n.sqrt(n.sum(segment_lengths * segment_lengths,
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    513	                                       axis=1))
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    514	        # if include_closure:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    515	        #     max_segment_length = n.max(segment_lengths)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    516	        # else:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    517	        max_segment_length = n.max(segment_lengths[:-1])
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    518	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    519	        numtries = len(points) - 3
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    520	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    521	        crossings = []
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    522	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    523	        jump_mode = {'count_every_jump': 1, 'use_max_jump': 2,
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    524	                     'naive': 3}[mode]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    525	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    526	        for i in range(len(points)-2):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    527	            if self.verbose:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    528	                if i % 100 == 0:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    529	                    self._vprint('\ri = {} / {}'.format(i, numtries),
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    530	                                 False)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    531	            v0 = points[i]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    532	            dv = points[(i+1) % len(points)] - v0
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    533	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    534	            s = points[i+2:]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    535	            vnum = i
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    536	            compnum = i+2
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    537	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    538	            crossings.extend(helpers_module.find_crossings(
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    539	                v0, dv, s, segment_lengths[compnum:],
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    540	                vnum, compnum,
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    541	                max_segment_length,
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    542	                jump_mode
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    543	                ))
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    544	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    545	        if include_closure:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    546	            closure_segment_length = segment_lengths[-1]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    547	            max_segment_length = n.max([closure_segment_length,
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    548	                                        max_segment_length])
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    549	            v0 = points[-1]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    550	            dv = points[0] - v0
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    551	            s = points[1:-1]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    552	            vnum = len(points) - 1
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    553	            compnum = 1
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    554	            crossings.extend(helpers_module.find_crossings(
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    555	                v0, dv, s, segment_lengths[compnum:],
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    556	                vnum, compnum,
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    557	                max_segment_length,
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    558	                jump_mode))
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    559	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    560	        self._vprint('\n{} crossings found\n'.format(len(crossings) / 2))
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    561	        crossings.sort(key=lambda s: s[0])
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    562	        crossings = n.array(crossings)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    563	        self._crossings = crossings
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    564	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    565	        return crossings
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    566	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    567	    def planar_writhe(self, **kwargs):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    568	        '''
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    569	        Returns the current planar writhe of the knot; the signed sum
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    570	        of crossings of the current projection.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    571	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    572	        The 'true' writhe is the average of this quantity over all
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    573	        projection directions, and is available from the :meth:`writhe`
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    574	        method.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    575	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    576	        Parameters
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    577	        ----------
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    578	        **kwargs :
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    579	            These are passed directly to :meth:`raw_crossings`.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    580	        '''
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    581	        crossings = self.raw_crossings(**kwargs)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    582	        return n.sum(crossings[:, 3]) / 2.

[tool-2bd] read_file ->    684	        '''
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    685	        Returns a :class:`~pyknotid.representations.gausscode.GaussCode`

[tool-2bd] read_file ->    767	    def plot_projection(self, with_crossings=True, mark_start=False,
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    768	                        fig_ax=None, show=True, mark_points=False):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    769	        '''Plots a 2D diagram of the knot projected along the current
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    770	        z-axis. The crossings, and start point of the curve, can
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    771	        optionally be marked.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    772	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    773	        The projection is drawn using matplotlib.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    774	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    775	        .. image:: example_knot_projection_9_5.png
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    776	           :align: center
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    777	           :scale: 50%
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    778	           :alt: An example knot projection.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    779	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    780	        Parameters
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    781	        ----------
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    782	        with_crossings : bool
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    783	            If True, marks the location of each self-intersection in
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    784	            projection. Defaults to True.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    785	        mark_start : bool
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    786	            If True, marks the first point of the curve. Default to False.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    787	        fig_ax : tuple
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    788	            A 2-tuple of the matplotlib (fig, ax) to use, or None
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    789	            to create a new pair.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    790	        show : bool
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    791	            If True, opens a new window showing the drawing. Defaults
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    792	            to True.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    793	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    794	        Returns
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    795	        -------
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    796	        A 2-tuple of the matplotlib (fig, ax) used for the drawing.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    797	        '''
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    798	        points = self.points
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    799	        crossings = None
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    800	        plot_crossings = []
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    801	        if with_crossings:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    802	            crossings = self.raw_crossings()
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    803	            plot_crossings = []
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    804	            for crossing in crossings:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    805	                x, y, over, orientation = crossing
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    806	                xint = int(x)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    807	                r = points[xint]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    808	                dr = points[(xint+1) % len(points)] - r
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    809	                plot_crossings.append(r + (x-xint) * dr)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    810	        fig, ax = plot_projection(points,
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    811	                                  crossings=n.array(plot_crossings),
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    812	                                  mark_start=mark_start,
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    813	                                  mark_points=mark_points,
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    814	                                  fig_ax=fig_ax,
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    815	                                  show=show)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    816	        return fig, ax

[tool-2bd] read_file ->    870	    def octree_simplify(self, runs=1, plot=False, rotate=True,
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    871	                        obey_knotting=True, **kwargs):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    872	        '''
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    873	        Simplifies the curve via the octree reduction of
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    874	        :module:`pyknotid.simplify.octree`.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    875	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    876	        Parameters
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    877	        ----------
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    878	        runs : int
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    879	            The number of times to run the octree simplification.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    880	            Defaults to 1.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    881	        plot : bool
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    882	            Whether to plot the curve after each run. Defaults to False.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    883	        rotate : bool
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    884	            Whether to rotate the space curve before each run. Defaults
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    885	            to True as this can make things much faster.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    886	        obey_knotting : bool
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    887	            Whether to not let the line pass through itself. Defaults to
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    888	            True as this is always what you want for a closed curve.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    889	        **kwargs
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    890	            Any remaining kwargs are passed to the
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    891	            :class:`pyknotid.simplify.octree.OctreeCell`
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    892	            constructor.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    893	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    894	        '''
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    895	        from pyknotid.simplify.octree import OctreeCell
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    896	        for i in range(runs):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    897	            if len(self.points) > 30:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    898	                self._vprint('\rRun {} of {}, {} points remain'.format(
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    899	                    i, runs, len(self.points)))
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    900	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    901	            if rotate:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    902	                rot_mat = get_rotation_matrix(n.random.random(3))
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    903	                self._apply_matrix(rot_mat)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    904	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    905	            oc = OctreeCell.from_single_line(self.points, **kwargs)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    906	            oc.simplify(obey_knotting)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    907	            self._recent_octree = oc
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    908	            self.points = oc.get_single_line()
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    909	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    910	            if rotate:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    911	                self._apply_matrix(rot_mat.T)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    912	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    913	            if plot:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    914	                self.plot()
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    915	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    916	        self._vprint('\nReduced to {} points'.format(len(self.points)))
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    917

[tool-2bd] read_file ->    938	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    939	    def radius_of_gyration_2(self):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    940	        rog_squared = 0.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    941	        for i, point in enumerate(self.points):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    942	            for next_point in self.points[i+1:]:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    943	                # next_point = self.points[i+1]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    944	                rog_squared += np.sum((next_point - point)**2)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    945	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    946	        rog_squared /= len(self.points)**2
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    947	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    948	        return np.sqrt(rog_squared)

[tool-2bd] read_file ->    954	    def reparameterised(self, mode='arclength', num_points=None,
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    955	                        interpolation='linear'):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    956	        '''
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    957	        Returns a new :class:`SpaceCurve` where new points have been selected
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    958	        by interpolating the current ones.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    959	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    960	        .. warning:: This doesn't do what you expect! The new segments
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    961	                     will probably not all be separated by the right amount
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    962	                     in terms of the new parameterisation.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    963	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    964	        Parameters
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    965	        ----------
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    966	        mode : str
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    967	            The function to reparameterise by. Defaults to 'arclength',
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    968	            which is currently the only option.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    969	        num_points : int
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    970	            The number of points in the new parameterisation. Defaults
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    971	            to None, which means the same as the current number.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    972	        interpolation : str
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    973	            The type of interpolation to use, passed directly to the
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    974	            ``kind`` option of ``scipy.interpolate.interp1d``. Defaults
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    975	            to 'linear', and other options have not been tested.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    976	        '''
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    977	        from scipy.interpolate import interp1d
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    978	        indices = self._new_indices_by_arclength(num_points)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    979	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    980	        interp_xs = interp1d(range(len(self.points)+1),
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    981	                             n.hstack((self.points[:, 0],
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    982	                                      self.points[:, 0][:1])))
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    983	        interp_ys = interp1d(range(len(self.points)+1),
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    984	                             n.hstack((self.points[:, 1],
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    985	                                      self.points[:, 1][:1])))
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    986	        interp_zs = interp1d(range(len(self.points)+1),
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    987	                             n.hstack((self.points[:, 2],
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    988	                                      self.points[:, 2][:1])))
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    989	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    990	        new_points = n.zeros((len(indices), 3), dtype=n.float)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    991	        new_points[:, 0] = interp_xs(indices)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    992	        new_points[:, 1] = interp_ys(indices)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    993	        new_points[:, 2] = interp_zs(indices)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    994	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    995	        return SpaceCurve(new_points)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    996	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    997	    def _new_indices_by_arclength(self, number, step=None, gap=0):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    998	        if number is None:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->    999	            number = len(self.points)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1000	        total_arclength = self.arclength()
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1001	        if step is None:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1002	            arclengths = n.linspace(0, total_arclength - gap, number+1)[:-1]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1003	        else:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1004	            arclengths = n.arange(0, total_arclength - gap, step)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1005	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1006	        arclengths[0] += 0.000001
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1007	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1008	        points = self.points
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1009	        segment_arclengths = self.segment_arclengths()
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1010	        cumulative_arclength = n.hstack([[0.], n.cumsum(segment_arclengths)])
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1011	        total_arclength = self.arclength()
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1012	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1013	        indices = []
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1014	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1015	        for arclength in arclengths:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1016	            first_greater_index = n.argmax(cumulative_arclength > arclength)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1017	            last_lower_index = (first_greater_index - 1) % len(points)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1018	            arclength_below = cumulative_arclength[last_lower_index]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1019	            step_arclength = segment_arclengths[last_lower_index]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1020	            step_fraction = (arclength - arclength_below) / step_arclength

[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1033	    def smooth(self, repeats=1, periodic=True, window_len=10,
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1034	               window='hanning'):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1035	        '''Smooths each of the x, y and z components of self.points by
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1036	        convolving with a window of the given type and size.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1037	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1038	        .. warning:: This is *not* topologically safe, it can change
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1039	                     the knot type of the curve. For topologically
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1040	                     safe reduction, see :meth:`octree_simplify`.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1041	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1042	        Parameters
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1043	        ----------
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1044	        repeats : int
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1045	            Number of times to run the smoothing algorithm. Defaults to 1.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1046	        periodic : bool
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1047	            If True, the convolution window wraps around the curve.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1048	            Defaults to True.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1049	        window_len : int
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1050	            Width of the convolution window. Defaults to 10.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1051	            Passed to :func:`pyknotid.spacecurves.smooth.smooth`.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1052	        window : string
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1053	            The type of convolution window. Defaults to 'hanning'.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1054	            Passed to :func:`pyknotid.spacecurves.smooth.smooth`.
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1055	        '''
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1056	        points = self.points
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1057	        if periodic:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1058	            points = n.vstack((points[-(window_len + 1):],
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1059	                               points,
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1060	                               points[:(window_len + 1)]))
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1061	        else:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1062	            start = points[0]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1063	            end = points[-1]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1064	        if len(points) > window_len:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1065	            for i in range(repeats):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1066	                points[:, 0] = smooth(points[:, 0], window_len, window)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1067	                points[:, 1] = smooth(points[:, 1], window_len, window)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1068	                points[:, 2] = smooth(points[:, 2], window_len, window)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1069	                if not periodic:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1070	                    points[0] = start
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1071	                    points[-1] = end
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1072	        if periodic:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1073	            points = points[(window_len + 1):-(window_len + 1)]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1074	        self.points = points
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1075	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1076	    def simplify_straight_segments(self, closed=False):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1077	        '''Replaces successive curve segments with identical tangents with a
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1078	        single longer segment.'''
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1079	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1080	        points = self.points
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1081	        ts = np.roll(points, -1, axis=0) - points
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1082	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1083	        keep_points = np.ones(len(points), dtype=np.bool)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1084	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1085	        ts_range = ts[:-2] if not closed else ts
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1086	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1087	        for i1, t1 in enumerate(ts_range):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1088	            t1 /= np.sqrt(np.sum(t1**2))
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1089	            t2 = ts[(i1 + 1) % len(ts)]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1090	            t2 /= np.sqrt(np.sum(t2**2))
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1091	            if np.dot(t1, t2) > (1 - 10e-10):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1092	                keep_points[i1 + 1] = 0
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1093	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1094	        self.points = self.points[keep_points]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1095	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1096	    def curvatures(self, closed=True):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1097	        '''Returns curvatures at each vertex (or really line segment).'''
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1098	        points = self.points
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1099	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1100	        ts = np.roll(points, -1, axis=0) - points
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1101	        vs = np.sqrt(np.sum(ts*ts, axis=1))
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1102	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1103	        gamma_dd = 0.25 * (np.roll(points, -2, axis=0) + np.roll(points, 2, axis=0) - 2*points)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1104	        vns = 0.5 * (vs + np.roll(vs, 1))
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1105	        gamma_d = 0.5 * (np.roll(points, -1, axis=0) - np.roll(points, 1, axis=0))
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1106	        ans = 0.25 * (np.roll(vs, -1) + vs - np.roll(vs, 1) - np.roll(vs, 2))
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1107	        kappas = gamma_dd - np.einsum('i,ij->ij', (ans / vns), gamma_d)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1108	        kappas = np.sqrt(np.sum(kappas * kappas, axis=1))
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1109	        kappas = (1./vns**2) * kappas
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1110	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1111	        if not closed:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1112	            kappas[:3] = kappas[3]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1113	            kappas[-3:] = kappas[-4]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1114	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1115	        return kappas
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1116	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1117	    def sum_of_angles(self):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1118	        points = self.points
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1119	        segments = np.roll(points, -1, axis=0) - points
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1120	        segments_2 = np.roll(segments, -1, axis=0)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1121	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1122	        mags = np.sqrt(np.sum(segments * segments, axis=1))
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1123	        mags_2 = np.roll(mags, -1)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1124	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1125	        angles = np.arccos(np.sum(segments * segments_2, axis=1) / (mags * mags_2))
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1126	        return np.sum(angles)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1127	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1128	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1129	    def torsions(self, signed=False, closed=True):
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1130	        '''Returns torsions at each vertex.'''
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1131	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1132	        d = self.points
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1133	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1134	        ts = np.roll(d, -1, axis=0) - d
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1135	        vs = np.sqrt(np.sum(ts*ts, axis=1))
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1136	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1137	        gamma_dd = 0.25 * (np.roll(d, -2, axis=0) + np.roll(d, 2, axis=0) - 2*d)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1138	        vns = 0.5 * (vs + np.roll(vs, 1))
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1139	        gamma_d = 0.5 * (np.roll(d, -1, axis=0) - np.roll(d, 1, axis=0))
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1140	        ans = 0.25 * (np.roll(vs, -1) + vs - np.roll(vs, 1) - np.roll(vs, 2))
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1141	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1142	        kappas = gamma_dd - np.einsum('i,ij->ij', (ans / vns), gamma_d)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1143	        kappas = np.sum(kappas * kappas, axis=1)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1144	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1145	        gamma_ddd = 0.125 * (np.roll(d, -3, axis=0) - 3*np.roll(d, -1, axis=0) +
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1146	                            3*np.roll(d, 1, axis=0) - np.roll(d, 3, axis=0))
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1147	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1148	        numerator = np.sum(np.cross(gamma_d, gamma_dd) * gamma_ddd, axis=1)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1149	        denominator = vns**2 * np.abs(kappas)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1150	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1151	        torsions = numerator / denominator
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1152	        if not signed:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1153	            torsions = np.abs(torsions)
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1154	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1155	        if not closed:
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1156	            torsions[:3] = torsions[3]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1157	            torsions[-3:] = torsions[-4]
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1158	
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1159	        return torsions
[tool-2bd] read_file ->   1160

[tool-905] read_file ->    190	    def simplify(self, obey_knotting=True):

[tool-905] read_file ->    192	        Performs a suitable action depending on the segments in the cell:
[tool-905] read_file ->    193	        - if 1 segment, checks the total angle and reduces to a straight

[tool-905] read_file ->    226	            elif s2.next == s1.prev:

[tool-905] read_file ->    387	class Handle(object):
[tool-905] read_file ->    388	    '''Simply stores a next and previous LineSegment, with a method to
[tool-905] read_file ->    389	    reconstruct the line by walking along the segments.
[tool-905] read_file ->    390	
[tool-905] read_file ->    391	    The Handle is like a LineSegment with no point data - all it does is
[tool-905] read_file ->    392	    sit in the line keeping track of its next and prev segments (which
[tool-905] read_file ->    393	    may be modified by the cut_at method of those LineSegments). Since
[tool-905] read_file ->    394	    it will never change itself, it can be used later to rejoin all these
[tool-905] read_file ->    395	    LineSegments which may have been created arbitrarily deep in the
[tool-905] read_file ->    396	    decomposition, returning the same line that corresponds to its input!
[tool-905] read_file ->    397	    '''
[tool-905] read_file ->    398	
[tool-905] read_file ->    399	    def __init__(self, next=None, prev=None, identifier=None):
[tool-905] read_file ->    400	        self.next = next  
[tool-905] read_file ->    401	        self.prev = prev  
[tool-905] read_file ->    402	        self.identifier = identifier
[tool-905] read_file ->    403	
[tool-905] read_file ->    404	    def reconstruct_line(self, remove_unnecessary_jumps=True):
[tool-905] read_file ->    405	        '''Returns the line stored by the handle, by walking along it until
[tool-905] read_file ->    406	        returning to the Handle or reaching a segment with no next entry.
[tool-905] read_file ->    407	
[tool-905] read_file ->    408	        If ``remove_unnecessary_jumps`` is True, will remove jumps connected
[tool-905] read_file ->    409	        by a trivial segment along the periodic cell wall.
[tool-905] read_file ->    410	        '''
[tool-905] read_file ->    411	        comp_segs = [self.next]
[tool-905] read_file ->    412	        current = self.next
[tool-905] read_file ->    413	        while current.next is not self and current.next is not None:
[tool-905] read_file ->    414	            current = current.next
[tool-905] read_file ->    415	            comp_segs.append(current)
[tool-905] read_file ->    416	
[tool-905] read_file ->    417	        if remove_unnecessary_jumps:
[tool-905] read_file ->    418	            invalid_indices = []
[tool-905] read_file ->    419	            for i, segment in enumerate(comp_segs):
[tool-905] read_file ->    420	                if i not in invalid_indices:
[tool-905] read_file ->    421	                    segment2 = comp_segs[(i+1) % len(comp_segs)]
[tool-905] read_file ->    422	                    segment3 = comp_segs[(i+1) % len(comp_segs)]
[tool-905] read_file ->    423	                    if (isinstance(segment, CellJumpSegment) and
[tool-905] read_file ->    424	                        isinstance(segment3, CellJumpSegment) and
[tool-905] read_file ->    425	                        len(segment2.points) == 2):
[tool-905] read_file ->    426	                        invalid_indices.append(i)
[tool-905] read_file ->    427	
[tool-905] read_file ->    428	            comp_segs = [seg for i, seg in enumerate(comp_segs) if
[tool-905] read_file ->    429	                         i not in invalid_indices]
[tool-905] read_file ->    430	
[tool-905] read_file ->    431	        return resample(n.vstack([seg.points[:-1] for
[tool-905] read_file ->    432	                                  seg in comp_segs] +
[tool-905] read_file ->    433	                                 [comp_segs[0].points[:1] +
[tool-905] read_file ->    434	                                  0.00001]))
[tool-905] read_file ->    435	
[tool-905] read_file ->    436	    def get_line_components(self):
[tool-905] read_file ->    437	        '''Returns a list of all the LineSegments in the line with
[tool-905] read_file ->    438	        this handle.'''

[tool-905] read_file ->    441	        while current.next is not self and current.next is not None:
[tool-905] read_file ->    442	            current = current.next
[tool-905] read_file ->    443	            component_segments.append(current)
[tool-905] read_file ->    444	        return component_segments

[tool-905] read_file ->    467	        '''
[tool-905] read_file ->    468	
[tool-905] read_file ->    469	        cut_x, cut_y, cut_z = cut_planes
[tool-905] read_file ->    470	        p = self.points[0] + 0.51123*(self.points[1] - self.points[0])

[tool-905] read_file ->    478	                return 'fll'

[tool-905] read_file ->    481	            else:

[tool-905] read_file ->    483	        elif z <= cut_z:
[tool-905] read_file ->    484	            if x > cut_x and y >= cut_y:
[tool-905] read_file ->    485	                return 'bur'
[tool-905] read_file ->    486	            elif x > cut_x and y <= cut_y:

[tool-905] read_file ->    497	    def cut_at(self, cuts):
[tool-905] read_file ->    498	        '''Returns a list of new LineSegments, resulting from cutting self at
[tool-905] read_file ->    499	        the given cut planes.
[tool-905] read_file ->    500	        '''
[tool-905] read_file ->    501	        points = self.points.copy()
[tool-905] read_file ->    502	        crude_segs = line_to_segments(points, cuts, join_ends=False)
[tool-905] read_file ->    503	        segs = crude_segs_to_segs(crude_segs, self.identifier)
[tool-905] read_file ->    504	
[tool-905] read_file ->    505	        # crude_segs_to_segs assumes the segments form a loop
[tool-905] read_file ->    506	        # here we instead connect the first to self.prev, and
[tool-905] read_file ->    507	        # the last to self.next
[tool-905] read_file ->    508	        segs[0].prev = self.prev
[tool-905] read_file ->    509	        self.prev.next = segs[0]
[tool-905] read_file ->    510	        segs[-1].next = self.next
[tool-905] read_file ->    511	        self.next.prev = segs[-1]
[tool-905] read_file ->    512	
[tool-905] read_file ->    513	        return segs

[tool-905] read_file ->    515	    def replace_with_straight_line(self):
[tool-905] read_file ->    516	        '''
[tool-905] read_file ->    517	        Replaces self.points with a straight line (i.e. just the first
[tool-905] read_file ->    518	        and last vertices).
[tool-905] read_file ->    519	
[tool-905] read_file ->    520	        This is only called when only one segment lies in a quadcell,
[tool-905] read_file ->    521	        therefore components can be cut out safely without worrying
[tool-905] read_file ->    522	        about any other parts of the full curve.'''
[tool-905] read_file ->    523	
[tool-905] read_file ->    524	        if self.next is not self.prev:
[tool-905] read_file ->    525	            self.points = n.vstack((self.points[0], self.points[-1]))
[tool-905] read_file ->    526	        else:  # if the line is a loop, just cut out most points
[tool-905] read_file ->    527	            self.points = self.points[::int(len(self.points)/3.)]

[tool-905] read_file ->    537	def line_to_segments(line, cuts=None, join_ends=True):
[tool-905] read_file ->    538	    '''Takes a line (set of points), a list of cut planes in
[tool-905] read_file ->    539	    x, y, z, and a parameter to decide whether the line
[tool-905] read_file ->    540	    joining the first and last point should also be cut.
[tool-905] read_file ->    541	
[tool-905] read_file ->    542	    Returns a list of shorter lines resulting from cutting at
[tool-905] read_file ->    543	    all these cut planes.'''
[tool-905] read_file ->    544	
[tool-905] read_file ->    545	    line = line.copy()
[tool-905] read_file ->    546	
[tool-905] read_file ->    547	    if cuts is None:
[tool-905] read_file ->    548	        xmin = n.min(line[:,0]) - 1
[tool-905] read_file ->    549	        xmax = n.max(line[:,0]) + 1
[tool-905] read_file ->    550	        ymin = n.min(line[:,1]) - 1
[tool-905] read_file ->    551	        ymax = n.max(line[:,1]) + 1
[tool-905] read_file ->    552	        zmin = n.min(line[:,2]) - 1
[tool-905] read_file ->    553	        zmax = n.max(line[:,2]) + 1
[tool-905] read_file ->    554	
[tool-905] read_file ->    555	        cut_x = (xmax + xmin) / 2.
[tool-905] read_file ->    556	        cut_y = (ymax + ymin) / 2.
[tool-905] read_file ->    557	        cut_z = (zmin + zmax) / 2.
[tool-905] read_file ->    558	    else:
[tool-905] read_file ->    559	        cut_x, cut_y, cut_z = cuts
[tool-905] read_file ->    560	
[tool-905] read_file ->    561	    # Cut the line wherever it passes through a quad cell boundary
[tool-905] read_file ->    562	    segments = []
[tool-905] read_file ->    563	    cut_i = 0
[tool-905] read_file ->    564	    for i in range(len(line)-1):
[tool-905] read_file ->    565	        cur = line[i]
[tool-905] read_file ->    566	        nex = line[i+1]
[tool-905] read_file ->    567	
[tool-905] read_file ->    568	        dv = nex - cur
[tool-905] read_file ->    569	        dx, dy, dz = dv
[tool-905] read_file ->    570	
[tool-905] read_file ->    571	        cross_cut_x = n.sign(cur[0] - cut_x) != n.sign(nex[0] - cut_x)
[tool-905] read_file ->    572	        cross_cut_y = n.sign(cur[1] - cut_y) != n.sign(nex[1] - cut_y)
[tool-905] read_file ->    573	        cross_cut_z = n.sign(cur[2] - cut_z) != n.sign(nex[2] - cut_z)
[tool-905] read_file ->    574	
[tool-905] read_file ->    575	        if cross_cut_x and cross_cut_y and cross_cut_z:
[tool-905] read_file ->    576	            x_cut_pos = -1 * (cur[0]-cut_x)/dx
[tool-905] read_file ->    577	            y_cut_pos = -1 * (cur[1]-cut_y)/dy
[tool-905] read_file ->    578	            z_cut_pos = -1 * (cur[2]-cut_z)/dz
[tool-905] read_file ->    579	            order = n.sort((x_cut_pos, y_cut_pos, z_cut_pos))
[tool-905] read_file ->    580	            # assert 0 < x_cut_pos < 1 and 0 < y_cut_pos < 1 and 0 < z_cut_pos < 1
[tool-905] read_file ->    581	            join_point_1 = cur + order[0]*dv
[tool-905] read_file ->    582	            join_point_2 = cur + order[1]*dv
[tool-905] read_file ->    583	            join_point_3 = cur + order[2]*dv
[tool-905] read_file ->    584	            first_seg = n.vstack((line[cut_i:(i+1)].copy(), join_point_1))
[tool-905] read_file ->    585	            second_seg = n.vstack((join_point_1, join_point_2))
[tool-905] read_file ->    586	            third_seg = n.vstack((join_point_2, join_point_3))
[tool-905] read_file ->    587	            line[i] = join_point_3
[tool-905] read_file ->    588	            cut_i = i
[tool-905] read_file ->    589	            segments.append(first_seg)
[tool-905] read_file ->    590	            segments.append(second_seg)
[tool-905] read_file ->    591	            segments.append(third_seg)
[tool-905] read_file ->    592	        elif cross_cut_x and cross_cut_y:

[tool-905] read_file ->    594	            y_cut_pos = -1 * (cur[1]-cut_y)/dy

[tool-905] read_file ->    604	        elif cross_cut_x and cross_cut_z:

[tool-905] read_file ->    669	def crude_segs_to_segs(csegs, identifier=None):
[tool-905] read_file ->    670	    '''Takes a list of line segments and makes them into LineSegment
[tool-905] read_file ->    671	    instances, under the assumption that the last is joined to the
[tool-905] read_file ->    672	    first. The segments are labelled by the passed identifier.
[tool-905] read_file ->    673	    '''
[tool-905] read_file ->    674	    segs = []
[tool-905] read_file ->    675	    for s in csegs:
[tool-905] read_file ->    676	        s = LineSegment(s, identifier=identifier)
[tool-905] read_file ->    677	        segs.append(s)
[tool-905] read_file ->    678	    for i in range(len(segs)):
[tool-905] read_file ->    679	        cur = segs[i]
[tool-905] read_file ->    680	        prev = segs[(i-1) % len(segs)]
[tool-905] read_file ->    681	        next = segs[(i+1) % len(segs)]
[tool-905] read_file ->    682	        cur.prev = prev
[tool-905] read_file ->    683	        cur.next = next
[tool-905] read_file ->    684	    return segs

[tool-905] read_file ->    687	def angle_exceeds(ps, val=2*n.pi, include_closure=True):
[tool-905] read_file ->    688	    '''Returns True if the sum of angles along ps exceeds
[tool-905] read_file ->    689	    val, else False.
[tool-905] read_file ->    690	
[tool-905] read_file ->    691	    If include_closure, includes the angles with the line closing
[tool-905] read_file ->    692	    the end and start points.
[tool-905] read_file ->    693	    '''
[tool-905] read_file ->    694	    angle = 0.
[tool-905] read_file ->    695	    nex = ps[0]
[tool-905] read_file ->    696	    nex2 = ps[1]
[tool-905] read_file ->    697	    dv2 = nex2-nex
[tool-905] read_file ->    698	    dv2 /= mag(dv2)
[tool-905] read_file ->    699	    checks = range(len(ps)) if include_closure else range(len(ps)-2)
[tool-905] read_file ->    700	    for i in checks:
[tool-905] read_file ->    701	        cur = nex
[tool-905] read_file ->    702	        nex = nex2
[tool-905] read_file ->    703	        nex2 = ps[(i+2) % len(ps)]
[tool-905] read_file ->    704	        dv = dv2
[tool-905] read_file ->    705	        dv2 = nex2-nex
[tool-905] read_file ->    706	        dv2 /= mag(dv2)
[tool-905] read_file ->    707	        increment = angle_between(dv, dv2)
[tool-905] read_file ->    708	        if n.isnan(increment):
[tool-905] read_file ->    709	            return True
[tool-905] read_file ->    710	        angle += increment
[tool-905] read_file ->    711	        if angle > val:
[tool-905] read_file ->    712	            return True
[tool-905] read_file ->    713	    assert not n.isnan(angle)
[tool-905] read_file ->    714	    return False
[tool-905] read_file ->    715	
[tool-905] read_file ->    716	def angle_between(v1, v2):
[tool-905] read_file ->    717	    '''Returns angle between v1 and v2, assuming they are normalised to 1.'''
[tool-905] read_file ->    718	    # clip becaus v1.dot(v2) may exceed 1 due to floating point
[tool-905] read_file ->    719	    return n.arccos(n.clip(n.abs(v1.dot(v2)), 0., 1.))
[tool-905] read_file ->    720	
[tool-905] read_file ->    721	
[tool-905] read_file ->    722	def mag(v):
[tool-905] read_file ->    723	    return n.sqrt(v.dot(v))
[tool-905] read_file ->    724

[tool-905] read_file ->    745	            ful.append(seg)
[tool-905] read_file ->    746	        elif cell == 'bur':

[tool-905] read_file ->    750	        elif cell == 'bll':

[tool-905] read_file ->    752	        elif cell == 'bul':

[tool-905] read_file ->    758	def resample(points):
[tool-905] read_file ->    759	    '''Takes a set of points, and cuts out the middle ones if they form
[tool-905] read_file ->    760	    part of a straight line, or if they are identical to the previous
[tool-905] read_file ->    761	    point.'''
[tool-905] read_file ->    762	    if len(points) < 2:
[tool-905] read_file ->    763	        return points
[tool-905] read_file ->    764	
[tool-905] read_file ->    765	    changing = True
[tool-905] read_file ->    766	    while changing:
[tool-905] read_file ->    767	        changing = False
[tool-905] read_file ->    768	        length = len(points)
[tool-905] read_file ->    769	        points = points.copy()
[tool-905] read_file ->    770	        keep = n.ones(len(points), dtype=bool)
[tool-905] read_file ->    771	        cur = points[0]
[tool-905] read_file ->    772	        nex = points[1]
[tool-905] read_file ->    773	        dv2 = nex-cur
[tool-905] read_file ->    774	        dv2 /= mag(dv2)
[tool-905] read_file ->    775	
[tool-905] read_file ->    776	        for i in range(1, len(points) - 1):
[tool-905] read_file ->    777	            prev = cur
[tool-905] read_file ->    778	            cur = nex
[tool-905] read_file ->    779	            nex = points[i+1]
[tool-905] read_file ->    780	            if n.all(n.less(n.abs(cur-nex), 0.0001)):

Now let me understand the key Numpy compatibility issues:

1. The pyx files use `cimport numpy as n` — this is the Cython numpy API. With Cython 3.x and NumPy 2.x, this generally works if Cython >= 3.0.10 (I need to check). Actually, NumPy 2.0 removed the old C API functions that Cython 0.29.x generated code used. Cython 3.0.x has been updated for NumPy 2.0 support. The `cimport numpy` uses the `numpy/__init__.pxd` from numpy's package which ships a compatible pxd for Cython.

Let me check numpy 2.3.0's bundled pxd — it should support Cython 3.x.

2. The `setup.py` uses `distutils.extension.Extension` which is removed in Python 3.12+. Since we're on Python 3.13, we need to change to `setuptools.Extension`.

3. The `numpy` C API: when Cython generates the C code using old numpy C API (#define NPY_NO_DEPRECATED_API etc.), the code may fail to compile with NumPy 2.x because of deprecated API usage. However, Cython 3.x generates code using the modern API variants, but the `.pyx` files may use deprecated attributes. Actually, when using `cimport numpy`, Cython generates calls like `__Pyx_SAFE_DOWNCAST` etc. and uses `PyArray_...` API functions. Most old functions still work through deprecation shims (`NPY_DEPRECATED_...`)? Actually in NumPy 2.0, the old C API functions such as `PyArray_SimpleNew`, `PyArray_DescrFromType`, `PyArray_Check`, etc. were **removed**. Code compiled with older Cython (0.29.x) that relied on those would fail at import with unclear errors (undefined symbol) or fail at compile.

But the crux: with Cython 3.0.11/3.1+ and numpy 2.x, the compiled numpy C API should work. The user says they had to modify and compile from source because pip install from PyPI won't be compatible with numpy >= 2.0.

Important detail: pip install from PyPI for pyknotid includes pre-compiled wheels with old Cython 0.29-generated code, which won't work with numpy 2. Also, the sdist uses distutils which is broken on Python 3.13.

So my plan:
1. Install build prerequisites: cython (>=3.0.11 or newer), setuptools, wheel.
2. Fix setup.py:
   - Use `setuptools.Extension` instead of `distutils.extension.Extension`.
   - Possibly add `setup_requires`/`build_requires`.
3. Fix the pyx files if needed for compilation with Cython 3 and numpy 2. Notably:
   - `cimport numpy as n` - need `cimport numpy as cnp`? It should work.
   - Some pyx files use `long [:] indices = np.zeros(4, dtype=np.int)` — in Cython, `np.int` might not resolve. Actually `np.int` (Python-level) - with numpy 2, `np.int` alias was removed. But within Cython, `dtype=np.int` would be compiled as a Python object reference to `numpy.int`, which no longer exists in numpy 2! That's a runtime error. In ccomplexity.pyx lines: `indices = np.zeros(4, dtype=np.int)` and `np.zeros(..., dtype=np.int)`.

   Actually, in Cython `dtype=np.int` is a Python expression evaluated at runtime. `np.int` was removed in NumPy 2.0 (deprecated since 1.20). So `np.zeros(4, dtype=np.int)` raises AttributeError at runtime. We should change to `np.intp` or `np.int64` or `long`.

   Wait, but these are just used to create the memoryview `long [:] indices`. `indices = np.zeros(4, dtype=np.int)` creates an array of platform C long ints (windows int is 32-bit). The memoryview is `long [:]`. On Linux, np.int maps to np.int64 = C long. So `np.intp` or `np.int64` works.

   Let me search for `np.int` etc. in the pyx files.

4. After building, install into system python. Since it's the "system's global Python environment", we install with `pip install .` which will install into site-packages. We need the dependencies installed too? The runtime deps include networkx, planarity, peewee, sympy, vispy, appdirs, requests, tqdm. [tool-5c2] exec_command -> def alexander(representation, variable=-1, quadrant='lr', simplify=True,
[tool-5c2] exec_command ->               mode='python'):
[tool-5c2] exec_command ->     '''Calculates the Alexander polynomial of the given knot. The
[tool-5c2] exec_command ->     representation *must* have just one knot component, or the
[tool-5c2] exec_command ->     calculation will fail or potentially give bad results.
[tool-5c2] exec_command -> 
[tool-5c2] exec_command ->     The result is returned with whatever numerical precision the
[tool-5c2] exec_command ->     algorithm produces, it is not rounded.
[tool-5c2] exec_command -> 
[tool-5c2] exec_command ->     The given representation *must* be simplified (RM1 performed if
[tool-5c2] exec_command ->     possible) for this to work, otherwise the matrix has overlapping
[tool-5c2] exec_command ->     elements. This is so important that this function automatically
[tool-5c2] exec_command ->     calls
[tool-5c2] exec_command ->     :meth:`pyknotid.representations.gausscode.GaussCode.simplify`, you
[tool-5c2] exec_command ->     must disable this manually if you don't want to do it.
[tool-5c2] exec_command -> 
[tool-5c2] exec_command ->     .. note:: If 'maxima' or 'mathematica' is chosen as the mode, the
[tool-5c2] exec_command ->               variable will automatically be set to ``t``.
[tool-5c2] exec_command -> 
[tool-5c2] exec_command ->     .. note:: If the mode is 'cypari', the quadrant argument will be
[tool-5c2] exec_command ->               ignored and the upper-left quadrant always used.
[tool-5c2] exec_command -> 
[tool-5c2] exec_command ->     Parameters

Key observations:
- `invariants.py` imports `sympy` at module level (`import sympy as sym`). So sympy is needed for the README example.
- `visualise.py` imports `vispy` at module level. `spacecurve.py` imports `from pyknotid.visualise import plot_line, plot_projection`. So vispy is needed for importing pyknotid.spacecurves. That's a big dependency but we can install it.

The `np.zeros(4, dtype=np.int)` in ccomplexity may not have caused errors — wait, that's a runtime expression since `np.int` is evaluated at runtime? Actually inside a Cython module compiled C code, `np.zeros(4, dtype=np.int)` calls the Python function `numpy.zeros` with keyword `dtype=np.int`. `np.int` is looked up at runtime from the module attribute. In NumPy 2.0, `np.int` was removed. So when the function is called at runtime, it would raise AttributeError. Although no compile error. Let me test importing the modules and calling the functions.

- `n.complex`, `n.float`, `n.bool`, `n.int` are removed in NumPy 2.0.
- `np.int`, `np.float`, `np.bool`.

But many of these could be triggered only in specific code paths (catalogue, openknot, complex, periodic_knot). Also line 990 `dtype=n.float`, and 638 `np.float`. And `k.alexander_polynomial` goes through invariants.py which uses `n.complex`, `n.float` around line 137 etc. So we need to fix all these to make the README example and tests work.

For memoryview typed arrays, `float` dtype in numpy maps to float64. For `n.complex` → `complex`. For `n.bool` → `bool`. For `n.int` → `int`.
