The `cython_higher_order_writhe` function has an out-of-bounds on buffer access. Let me look at the function again:

```python
cpdef cython_higher_order_writhe(double [:, :] points,
                        double [:, :] contributions,
                        long [:] order):

    cdef long i1, i2, i3, i4
    cdef long [:] indices = np.zeros(4, dtype=np.int64)

    cdef double writhe = 0.0

    for i1 in range(len(points) - 3):
        print('\rcython i1', i1, len(points) - 4, end='')
        sys.stdout.flush()
        indices[0] = i1
        for i2 in range(i1 + 1, len(points) - 1):
            indices[1] = i2
            for i3 in range(i2 + 1, len(points) - 1):
                indices[2] = i3
                for i4 in range(i3 + 1, len(points) - 1):
                    indices[3] = i4

                    writhe += (contributions[indices[order[0]],
                                             indices[order[1]]] *
                               contributions[indices[order[2]],
                                             indices[order[3]]])
    print()

    return writhe
```

The out-of-bounds is `order[3]` when `order` is a `long[:]` with 4 elements (indices 0..3). Index is fine. But wait — the error is at line 34: `indices[order[3]]`. Hmm, `order` has length 4, so `order[3]` is valid. Unless my order array is wrong? I passed `np.array([1, 3, 2, 4])` which is length 4, so order[3] = 4. Then `indices[order[3]]` = `indices[4]` — that's out of bounds because `indices` has length 4! 

The `order` here is a permutation of (1, 2, 3, 4) by default from the calling code `higher_order_writhe_integral` with order=(1,3,2,4). Then `indices[order[0]]` means indices[1], which is fine, but `indices[order[3]]` means indices[4] which is out of bounds! 

Wait, this is in the original pyknotid code. Let me look at how it's called in complexity.py.

I see. In `writhing_matrix` (whose result feeds into `higher_order_writhe_integral`), `order` is converted: `order = [o - 1 for o in order]`. When called via `higher_order_writhe_integral(points, order=(1,3,2,4))`, it becomes `[0, 2, 1, 3]`. Then in cython `cython_higher_order_writhe`, `indices[order[0]]` = indices[0], ..., `indices[order[3]]` = indices[3], all fine. So in the proper invocation, `order` values are 0-based.

So in real usage, `order` is converted from 1-based to 0-based earlier (`order = [o - 1 for o in order]`), then `cython_higher_order_writhe(points, contributions, order)` is called with the 0-based order. My test passed a 1-based order directly, which caused out-of-bounds. That's a bug in my test, not in the package. Let me re-test with 0-based order.
My test wrongly passed 1-based `order`; the package converts it to 0-based before calling. Let me re-test properly:

Now it's clear. The modern `planarity.networkx_graph(pg)` returns:
- Node attributes: `vertex_position` (y), `vertex_start` (xb), `vertex_end` (xe)
- Edge attributes: `edge_position` (x), `edge_start` (yb), `edge_end` (ye)

The pyknotid code expects node `data['pos']` (y), `data['start']` (xb), `data['end']` (xe); edge `data['pos']` (x), `data['start']` (yb), `data['end']` (ye).

The simplest fix: In `pyknotid/representations/representation.py`, remap the attributes after `planarity.networkx_graph(pg)`. We can add a small adapter to rename:
- For each node `data`: if `'vertex_position' in data`, set `data['pos'] = data.pop('vertex_position')`, `data['start'] = data.pop('vertex_start')`, `data['end'] = data.pop('vertex_end')`.
- For each edge `data`: if `'edge_position' in data`, set `data['pos'] = data.pop('edge_position')`, `data['start'] = data.pop('edge_start')`, `data['end'] = data.pop('edge_end')`.

Let me verify the old planarity names used by pyknotid: In `_construct_planar_graph`:
- nodes: y = data['pos'], xb = data['start'], xe = data['end']
- edges: x = data['pos'], yb = data['start'], ye = data['end']

## Summary

### Problem
`pyknotid 0.5.3` was incompatible with the global Python 3.13.15 environment with **NumPy 2.3.0**:
- The PyPI wheels were built with old Cython/NumPy C API code and don't work with NumPy ≥2.0.
- `setup.py` used `distutils.extension.Extension`, which was **removed in Python 3.13**.
- The Cython sources used NumPy 2-removed aliases (`np.int`, `n.float`, `n.complex`, `n.bool`).
- `pyknotid/make/torus.py` used `fractions.gcd`, removed in Python 3.9+ (moved to `math`).
- The installed `planarity 1.0.0` exposes different attribute names (`vertex_position`, `edge_position`, …) than pyknotid expected (`pos`, `start`, `end`).

### Fixes made (in `/app/pyknotid`)
1. **`setup.py`** – replaced `distutils.extension.Extension` with `setuptools.Extension`.
2. **`pyproject.toml`** (new) – declared `setuptools`, `wheel`, `Cython>=3.0.11` and `numpy` as build-system requirements so `pip install .` always compiles the Cython extensions (Python 3.13 compatible).
3. **`pyknotid/spacecurves/ccomplexity.pyx`** – replaced removed `dtype=np.int` with `dtype=np.int64` (3 places) so the compiled `ccomplexity` functions work at runtime.
4. **NumPy 2 removed aliases** replaced with exact Python equivalents across:
   - `spacecurve.py` (`astype(n.float)` → `astype(float)`, `dtype=n.float` → `dtype=float`, `dtype=np.bool` → `dtype=bool`)
   - `invariants.py` (`n.complex`/`n.float` → `complex`/`float`)
   - `knot.py`, `openknot.py`, `periodiccell.py`, `gausscode.py`, `representation.py`, `dtnotation.py`, `octree.py`, `make/periodic_knot.py`
5. **`make/torus.py`** – `from fractions import gcd` → `from math import gcd`.
6. **`representations/representation.py`** – normalises `planarity.networkx_graph` output (`vertex_position/start/end`, `edge_position/start/end`) back to the `pos/start/end` names pyknotid expects, fixing `KeyError: 'pos'`.

### Build & install
```
cd /app/pyknotid
pip install .          # builds/installs into the global environment
```
The wheel built and installed successfully to `/usr/local/lib/python3.13/site-packages/pyknotid`, with compiled extensions:
- `pyknotid/spacecurves/chelpers.cpython-313-aarch64-linux-gnu.so`
- `pyknotid/spacecurves/ccomplexity.cpython-313-aarch64-linux-gnu.so`
- `pyknotid/cinvariants.cpython-313-aarch64-linux-gnu.so`
- `pyknotid/simplify/coctree.cpython-313-aarch64-linux-gnu.so`

### Verification (all against the installed package, run from `/tmp`)
- **README example** runs cleanly with NumPy 2.3.0:
  ```
  k = sp.Knot(mk.three_twist(num_points=100))
  out = k.alexander_polynomial(-1)   # → 6.999999999999999 (≈7)
  ```
  with all three requested imports (`chelpers`, `ccomplexity`, `cinvariants`) resolving to the compiled `.so` files.
- **Required tests pass** (the excluded `test_random_curves.py` [dead code] and `test_catalogue.py` were not run):
  - `tests/test_knot.py` + `tests/test_spacecurve.py` → **18 passed**
  - Covers invariants, identify, gauss code, octree simplify, writhes, `test_compiled_vs_python_find_crossings`, etc.
- Compiled `ccomplexity` (`cython_second_order_writhes`, `cython_second_order_writhes_no_basepoint`, `cython_higher_order_writhe`) and `cinvariants.vassiliev_degree_3` all execute correctly.

The original package structure is unchanged — only compatibility fixes were applied, plus a standard `pyproject.toml` for a reliable modern build.
